# SSAC27 foreperiod study: analysis results

Generated by `analysis/build_results.py` from `analysis/numbers.json`; do not edit by hand. **Status: FINAL for the checkpoint** on the data versions listed below (every artifact re-verified byte-for-byte with scripts/verify_producers.py). Analyses marked PRELIMINARY remain preliminary in the scientific sense. Simulated quantities are labelled SIMULATION. Mock data never enter this file.

Reproduce everything: `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis\run_all.py`; verify every registered artifact byte-for-byte: `.venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts\verify_producers.py producers.json`.

## Headline results (read this first)

Terminology: **Seiko Ready Time** = the official start-information system's 'Ready Time' (from the start waveform images). It is *not* the set-onset-to-gun foreperiod of the literature: broadcast-audio foreperiods exceed it by about half a second with scatter (section 2b). **Foreperiod** below means set onset to gun (broadcast audio) unless stated. **Hold** is the starter's set-to-gun interval in the literature sense.

1. **Data.** 6,408 valid starts, 836 races, 18 championships (1999-2025); Seiko Ready Time for 232 races (1,778 valid RTs).
2. **Ready Time vs mean RT (preliminary).** 0.32 (95% CI -0.15 to 0.80) ms per 100 ms longer Ready Time (minimum detectable 0.68); r-equivalent 0.035 (95% CI -0.016 to 0.087), which excludes |r| = 0.16 (Haugen et al. 2013). Correcting for Ready Time being a noisy proxy of the foreperiod (calibration factor 0.82 (95% CI 0.59 to 1.05), 22 audio-annotated races): r 0.039 (95% CI -0.018 to 0.098), which still excludes |r| = 0.16.
3. **Shape (preliminary).** Slope below each championship's median Ready Time -0.67 (95% CI -1.50 to 0.17), above it 1.22 (95% CI 0.44 to 2.00) ms per 100 ms; lowest-AIC model: M10 piecewise at champ. median; reciprocal-PDF effect 1.22 (95% CI 0.17 to 2.27) ms per SD (predicted sign, CI excludes 0); objective hazard 1.08 (95% CI -0.05 to 2.21) ms per SD (opposite sign, CI includes 0).
4. **Championship offsets.** Adjusted championship RT offsets span 33.9 ms (LRT chi2 = 648); championships carry 14.8% (95% CI 8.0 to 25.8) of RT variance. Not a changed detection threshold: at every championship with waveforms the detection line sits at the same point of the displayed force rise (median offset -4.2 ms), while force onsets span 35 ms (section 1); source not identified.
5. **Simulation (SIMULATION).** Legitimate starts scored < 0.100 s: 0.284 (95% CI 0.144 to 0.482) per 1,000 (men, ex-Gaussian, average championship). Across the 10th-90th percentile Ready Time the measured slope allows at most a 3.4-fold change (3.9-fold on the foreperiod scale after proxy correction); the interquartile range of championship offsets gives 18.4-fold.
6. **Power (simulation).** |r| = 0.16 needs 55-68 races at residual race ICC 0.05-0.10 (56 at the observed race ICC); athlete-level tests that ignore nesting have type I error 0.136 at ICC 0.10.

Figures for the abstract: ★ `analysis/figures/holds.png` and ★ `analysis/figures/fairness.png` (section Figures).

## Data corrections

- **2026-09-30, false-start classification (common.normalize_rt).** The false-start label regex matched inside the note "(not a false start)", so hurdle and lane disqualifications were counted as false starts and their legal RTs left the valid set; and the Fiore et al. file's RT 0.000 placeholders (DNS rows, and DQ rows whose negative RT was dropped) were treated as measured RTs. Fixed with regression tests (`analysis/tests/test_common_fs.py`). After the fix: 37 recorded false starts (27 with a measured RT) in 6,452 starts, 5.74 (95% CI 4.16 to 7.89) per 1,000; 6,408 valid starts. Every producer was rerun; every changed key (old and new value), the abstract placeholders and the pre-registered verdicts are listed in `notes/bugfix_rerun_diff.md`.

## 1. Descriptive results (RT data only)

*Source:* `analysis/outputs/descriptive.json` from `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis/descriptive.py --seed 20260928 --boot 1000 --fit-boot 200 --meet-boot 100 --min-starts 4 --out analysis/outputs/descriptive.json`  
*Data:* rt_athletes.csv@sha256:af44975b3e1a (4904 rows); rt_fiore.csv@sha256:dff5cb184d1b (2805 rows); races.csv@sha256:4f0f5097c6dc (625 rows)

- **Coverage:** 6,408 valid starts in 836 races from 18 championships, 1999–2025; 1,194 identified athletes. Valid start = RT 0.100–0.300 s, not a false start, not DNS; 7 RTs above 0.300 s excluded.
- **Median RT:** men 153.0 (95% CI 151.0 to 154.0) ms, women 156.0 (95% CI 155.0 to 157.0) ms (race-cluster bootstrap CIs).
- **Adjusted contrasts** (crossed random intercepts for championship, race and athlete): women − men 4.4 (95% CI 2.7 to 6.1) ms; final − first round -5.4 (95% CI -7.3 to -3.6) ms; semi-final − first round -2.7 (95% CI -4.0 to -1.5) ms; preliminary − first round 5.0 (95% CI 1.9 to 8.1) ms; 200 m − flat sprints 4.5 (95% CI 2.8 to 6.2) ms; hurdles − flat sprints -1.2 (95% CI -3.2 to 0.7) ms. Flat sprints = 100 m and indoor 60 m (the World Indoors have only 60 m / 60 m hurdles, so indoor differences sit in the championship effect).
- **Variance components (SD, ms):** championship 9.3 (95% CI 6.6 to 13.1), race 5.1 (95% CI 4.4 to 5.8), athlete 15.6 (95% CI 14.6 to 16.6), residual 15.0. Championships carry 14.82% (95% CI 8.02 to 25.77) of RT variance; within a championship the race ICC is 0.052 (95% CI 0.040 to 0.068) and athletes carry 49.24% (95% CI 46.10 to 52.39).
- **Championship offsets:** adjusted offsets span 33.9 ms, from WCH2022 (fastest-scoring) to WCH2025 (slowest); likelihood-ratio test for championship effects chi2 = 647.7, df = 17, p = 1.1e-126. RTs in the WA JSON and the official PDFs agree (checked by the data pipeline), so these offsets are in the official results. Detection-threshold check: the median position of the Seiko detection line relative to the displayed force onset is -4.2 ms at every championship with waveforms (WCH2022, WCH2023, WCH2025, WIC2024, WIC2025; range of medians 0.0 ms; 71.73% of 2108 lanes at the modal offset), while the median force onset after the gun spans 35.0 ms; the offset distributions still differ slightly in their upper quartile (Kruskal-Wallis p = 9.4e-14). So the offsets are not a changed detection threshold on the displayed trace; their source is not identified by these data.
- **Omega (Olympics) vs Seiko (Worlds):** -5.5 (95% CI -20.0 to 9.0) ms, a championship-level contrast resting on Omega: 2, Seiko: 8 championships; not interpretable as a vendor effect.
- **Trend:** 1.0 (95% CI -4.2 to 6.2) ms per decade (championship random effect).
  - rule era 2003-2009 minus pre-2003 (adjusted): 12.2 (95% CI -3.1 to 27.5) ms
  - rule era 2010+ minus pre-2003 (adjusted): 13.2 (95% CI -0.3 to 26.8) ms
- **Near the threshold:** share of valid RTs in [0.100, 0.110) s: men 0.576% (95% CI 0.345 to 0.839), women 0.511% (95% CI 0.271 to 0.751); below 0.120 s: men 2.97% (95% CI 2.31 to 3.71), women 2.08% (95% CI 1.55 to 2.66).
- **Recorded false starts:** 5.7 (95% CI 4.2 to 7.9) per 1,000 starts (Wilson CI); 4 of 27 recorded false starts with an RT fell in [0.090, 0.100) s, by championship: WCH2022 4.
- **Fitted P(RT < 0.100) per 1,000 gun-triggered starts (average championship, first round, flat sprint):** men: exgauss 0.28 (95% CI 0.14 to 0.48); slognorm 0.05 (95% CI 0.01 to 0.15); swald 0.05 (95% CI 0.01 to 0.17) | women: exgauss 0.09 (95% CI 0.05 to 0.17); slognorm 0.0029 (95% CI 0.0001 to 0.02); swald 0.0025 (95% CI 6.9e-05 to 0.02). These are left-truncated extrapolations; the family spread is the honest uncertainty.
- **Fitted P(RT < 0.100) per 1,000 gun-triggered starts (pooled over championships):** men: exgauss 0.66 (95% CI 0.36 to 1.24); slognorm 0.10 (95% CI 0.02 to 0.31); swald 0.10 (95% CI 0.02 to 0.30) | women: exgauss 0.39 (95% CI 0.20 to 0.79); slognorm 0.02 (95% CI 0.004 to 0.09); swald 0.02 (95% CI 0.003 to 0.07). These are left-truncated extrapolations; the family spread is the honest uncertainty.
- **Per-championship fitted P(RT < 0.100)** (ex-Gaussian, men): highest 5.44 (95% CI 1.22 to 15.86) per 1,000 at WCH2022, lowest 2e-06 (95% CI 7.8e-16 to 0.00087) per 1,000 at WCH2013.
- **Fastest-quartile athletes** (split-half selection, held-out starts): -11.2 ms vs all eligible athletes (106 of 421 athletes); fitted P(RT < 0.100) 1.11 (95% CI 0.26 to 3.30) per 1,000 starts.

## 2. Reaction time vs Seiko Ready Time (PRELIMINARY)

*Source:* `analysis/outputs/fp_models.json` from `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis/fp_models.py --seed 20260928 --fp-source seiko --exclude-fp-comps WIC2025 --boot 500 --perm 5000 --phi 0.26 --phi-pdf 0.21 --catch 0.05 --out analysis/outputs/fp_models.json`  
*Data:* rt_waveform.csv@sha256:3711f9b56ca6 (277 rows); rt_athletes.csv@sha256:af44975b3e1a (4904 rows); races.csv@sha256:4f0f5097c6dc (625 rows)

Ready Time is the official Seiko start-system interval; it is a noisy, offset proxy for the set-onset-to-gun foreperiod (section 2b), so slopes here are per 100 ms of Ready Time.

- **Sample:** 1,778 valid RTs (false starts and DNS excluded) in 232 races with a validated Seiko 'Ready Time' (22 after an aborted attempt). Ready Time median 1.601 s, SD 0.252 s (within-championship SD 0.229 s), range 0.806 to 2.395 s. Median Ready Time by championship: WCH2022 1.440 s, WCH2023 1.686 s, WCH2025 1.677 s, WIC2024 1.453 s (ANOVA F = 17.3, p = 3.7e-10). All models: championship fixed effects (RT offsets differ between championships), sex, round, event type, crossed random intercepts for race and athlete; ML fits.
- **Excluded:** WIC2025 Ready Times (38 races; median 3.25 s, far outside every other championship's range), treated as a suspected different 'Ready Time' definition until explained.
- **Linear:** 0.32 (95% CI -0.15 to 0.80) ms per 100 ms longer Ready Time (p = 0.179; race-cluster bootstrap 95% CI -0.23 to 0.76). r-equivalent 0.035 (95% CI -0.016 to 0.087). A Haugen-sized association (|r| = 0.16) is 1.47 ms per 100 ms on this sample's scale; the 95% CI excludes it in both directions. Minimum detectable slope at 80% power: 0.68 ms per 100 ms (|r| 0.074).
- **Shape:** slope below each championship's median Ready Time -0.67 (95% CI -1.50 to 0.17) ms per 100 ms, above it 1.22 (95% CI 0.44 to 2.00) ms per 100 ms (piecewise model; lit/review.md predicts above the mode: reciprocal PDF +, hazard -, fMTP about 0). Spline-predicted fastest Ready Time 1.39 (95% CI 1.06 to 2.12) s vs median 1.601 s. Spline vs linear LRT p = 0.071.
- **Which temporal-expectation account fits best** (dAIC vs no Ready Time term, negative = better; same data, ML): raw linear 0.19, spline -1.10, objective hazard -1.52, Weber-blurred subjective hazard 1.82 (session distribution 0.73), abort-adjusted hazard 1.94, blurred reciprocal PDF -3.19, fMTP-style blurred CDF -0.58, relative Ready Time -0.36, piecewise at the median -5.59. Lowest AIC: M10 piecewise at champ. median. Effects per SD of the predictor: reciprocal PDF 1.22 (95% CI 0.17 to 2.27) ms, subjective hazard 0.25 (95% CI -0.90 to 1.40) ms, objective hazard 1.08 (95% CI -0.05 to 2.21) ms, fMTP-style 0.89 (95% CI -0.19 to 1.96) ms. dAIC differences of 2 to 4 are weak-to-moderate evidence, and eleven models were compared.
- **Direction check against each account's prediction** (hazard and fMTP-style: longer expected -> faster, coefficient < 0; reciprocal PDF: less expected -> slower, coefficient > 0): h_obj_comp_z: opposite sign, CI includes 0; h_subj_comp_z: opposite sign, CI includes 0; h_subj_session_z: predicted sign, CI includes 0; h_abort_comp_z: opposite sign, CI includes 0; recip_pdf_comp_z: predicted sign, CI excludes 0; fmtp_cdf_comp_z: opposite sign, CI includes 0.
- **Sequential effect (athlete's previous start at the championship):** 0.72 (95% CI -0.14 to 1.58) ms per 100 ms that the previous Ready Time was longer, -0.52 (95% CI -1.38 to 0.34) ms per 100 ms that it was shorter (751 starts, 326 athletes; rounds are on different days).
- **Heterogeneity:** Ready Time x sex 0.62 (95% CI -0.18 to 1.42) ms per 100 ms (women minus men, p = 0.132); championship-specific linear slopes LRT p = 0.025. Excluding restarts: 0.33 (95% CI -0.20 to 0.85) ms per 100 ms.
- **Fast starts:** odds of a valid RT < 0.120 s per 100 ms longer Ready Time 0.99 (95% CI 0.90 to 1.10) (GEE, championship-adjusted, p = 0.920); within-championship permutation p = 0.829. Sub-0.100 s responses vs Ready Time cannot be estimated: every false-start race's waveform shows the restart, so the interval of the attempt that produced the false start is unknown (broadcast audio could supply it). Pooled bins in fp_models.json are confounded by championship; the within-championship terciles below are the valid comparison.

| WCH2022 | long | 23 | 160 | 1.750 | 144.7 | 15 | 9.4% |
| WCH2022 | mid | 22 | 159 | 1.450 | 136.5 | 28 | 17.6% |

## 2b. Seiko Ready Time vs broadcast-audio foreperiod (errors-in-variables check)

*Source:* `analysis/outputs/calibration.json` from `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis/calibration.py --seed 20260928 --boot 5000 --mc 20000 --fp-models analysis/outputs/fp_models.json --out analysis/outputs/calibration.json`  
*Data:* manual_annotations.csv@sha256:fe3736ebbb0f (36 rows); rt_waveform.csv@sha256:3711f9b56ca6 (277 rows); rt_athletes.csv@sha256:af44975b3e1a (4904 rows); races.csv@sha256:4f0f5097c6dc (625 rows)

- **Pairs:** 22 races with a clean manual broadcast annotation of the same attempt. Audio foreperiod (set onset to gun) minus Ready Time: mean 0.523 s, within-championship SD 0.112 s; by championship WCH2022 0.274 s, WCH2023 0.385 s, WCH2025 0.610 s. Pearson r(Ready Time, audio foreperiod) = 0.74.
- **Calibration factor** (within-championship slope of audio foreperiod on Ready Time): 0.82 (95% CI 0.59 to 1.05); with the end of voicing of 'Set' instead of its onset: 0.88. A factor below 1 means Ready Time exaggerates foreperiod differences, so RT slopes per unit of Ready Time are attenuated by that factor.
- **Corrected effect:** 0.40 (95% CI -0.17 to 1.02) ms per 100 ms of foreperiod; r-equivalent 0.039 (95% CI -0.018 to 0.098), which still excludes |r| = 0.16. Conservative bound (calibration factor at its lower 2.5% bootstrap bound): r from -0.021 to 0.113.
- **Direct fit on audio foreperiods** (small sample, reported separately): 1.02 (95% CI -0.39 to 2.43) ms per 100 ms (22 races, 174 RTs); too imprecise to add information.
- Assumptions: the calibration sample is representative of the Ready-Time sample (transportability), the proxy error is non-differential for RT, and the relation is linear within a championship. The pair sample is small and grows as the measurement pipeline annotates more races; rerun analysis/run_all.py to update.

## 2c. Automated broadcast-audio foreperiod pipeline: held-out validation (measurement pipeline)

*Source:* `analysis/measure/numbers.json` (measurement pipeline's generated numbers, merged verbatim; not in producers.json)  
*Data:* numbers.json@sha256:b87a6a30d106

- **Held-out clean starts:** 10. Automated vs blind manual annotation: gun onset MAE 6.17 ms (within 20 ms: 83.3%); 'Set' onset MAE 33.7 ms (within 20 ms: 60.0%, within 40 ms: 70.0%); foreperiod MAE 36.2 ms (within 40 ms: 70.0%; largest error 126.6 ms).
- These are the measurement pipeline's numbers (analysis/measure/README.md explains the pipeline); all `measure.*` keys are listed in Appendix B.

## 3. Power analysis (simulation, nested design)

*Source:* `analysis/outputs/power.json` from `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis/power_analysis.py --seed 20260928 --sims 10000 --m 8 --r 0.16 --validate-sims 200 --descriptive analysis/outputs/descriptive.json --out analysis/outputs/power.json`  
*Data:* rt_athletes.csv@sha256:af44975b3e1a (4904 rows); rt_fiore.csv@sha256:dff5cb184d1b (2805 rows); races.csv@sha256:4f0f5097c6dc (625 rows)

- **Races needed for 80% power** at |r| = 0.16 (8 athletes per race, two-sided alpha 0.05): 42 (residual race ICC 0), 55 (0.05), 68 (0.10), 94 (0.20). For 90%: 56, 73, 91, 126.
- **At the observed within-championship race ICC** (0.052): 56 races for 80% power.
- **Implied slope:** |r| = 0.16 corresponds to 2.23 ms per 100 ms with SD(RT) 22 ms and SD(hold) 0.158 s (range across SD assumptions in the table in power.json).
- **Ignoring nesting inflates false positives:** athlete-level OLS type I error 0.093 at ICC 0.05, 0.136 at 0.10, 0.203 at 0.20 (race-mean analysis: 0.055 at 0.10).
- **Minimum detectable |r| (80% power):** 0.102 with 100 races (ICC 0), 0.083 with 150 races; 0.133 / 0.107 at ICC 0.10.
- **Check vs statsmodels MixedLM** (N40_icc0.10): decision agreement 0.960 (power 0.475 race means vs 0.515 MixedLM).
- **Check vs statsmodels MixedLM** (N70_icc0.10): decision agreement 0.975 (power 0.830 race means vs 0.825 MixedLM).

## 4. Simulation study: hold-conditional false-start risk for legitimate starts (SIMULATION)

*Source:* `analysis/outputs/fairness.json` from `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis/fairness_sim.py --seed 20260928 --phi 0.25 --descriptive analysis/outputs/descriptive.json --fp-models analysis/outputs/fp_models.json --calibration analysis/outputs/calibration.json --out analysis/outputs/fairness.json`  
*Data:* rt_athletes.csv@sha256:af44975b3e1a (4904 rows); rt_fiore.csv@sha256:dff5cb184d1b (2805 rows); races.csv@sha256:4f0f5097c6dc (625 rows); rt_waveform.csv@sha256:3711f9b56ca6 (277 rows); manual_annotations.csv@sha256:fe3736ebbb0f (36 rows)

All quantities in this section are model outputs from fitted real RT distributions plus stated scenario inputs; none is an observed foreperiod effect.

- **Baseline:** at an average championship's calibration, 0.284 (95% CI 0.144 to 0.482) legitimate starts per 1,000 are scored below 0.100 s for men and 0.089 (95% CI 0.046 to 0.169) for women (ex-Gaussian); shifted lognormal 0.0504 (95% CI 0.0124 to 0.1505) (men), shifted Wald 0.0514 (95% CI 0.0105 to 0.1652) (men).
- **Measured effect:** within the 95% CI of the Ready-Time slope, moving from the 10th to the 90th percentile Seiko Ready Time changes that probability by at most 3.41-fold (men); on the foreperiod scale after the proxy correction (section 2b) at most 3.89-fold, or 4.92-fold with the calibration factor at its lower bound.
- **Measured shape (sensitivity):** with the fitted piecewise slopes, the probability at the 10th and 90th percentile Ready Times is 0.59 and 0.38 times its value at the median (men), i.e. risk would be highest near the typical interval.
- **Literature hold effects:** a Haugen-sized effect makes the risk 10.1 (95% CI 8.4 to 12.9) times higher at the 90th than at the 10th percentile hold if RT is faster after long holds, or 10.1 (95% CI 8.4 to 12.9) times higher at the 10th than at the 90th percentile if RT is slower after long holds; the hazard model calibrated to |r| = 0.16 gives 10.6 (95% CI 8.8 to 13.6)-fold; the Otsuka lab contrast would give 584 (95% CI 351 to 1,133)-fold.
- **Championship offsets** (applied as location shifts of the fitted distribution): moving between the 25th and 75th percentile championship offsets changes the probability 18.4-fold; between the fastest- and slowest-scoring championships 4,430-fold (WCH2022: 13.83 per 1,000; WCH2025: 0.0031 per 1,000).
- **Fastest-quartile athletes** (held-out starts): 1.11 (95% CI 0.26 to 3.30) per 1,000 with no hold effect; 1.58 (95% CI 0.45 to 4.14) (Haugen-sized, faster after long holds); 7.06 (95% CI 3.81 to 11.69) (Otsuka lab slope).
- **Starter policies (hazard model, men):** championship (Normal 1.78, SD 0.158) 0.312 per 1,000; predictable (Normal 1.78, SD 0.08) 0.378 per 1,000; variable (Normal 1.78, SD 0.30) 0.174 per 1,000; uniform 1.30-2.26 0.188 per 1,000; non-ageing (1.30 + exponential, mean 1.78) 0.074 per 1,000.
- **Foreperiod-aware threshold:** keeping the legitimate false-alarm rate constant would move the threshold by 9.1 ms between the 10th and 90th percentile holds under a Haugen-sized effect, versus the 33.9 ms spread of championship offsets.

**Fairness implication.** Within the range of Seiko Ready Times starters actually produce, the measured effect moves a legitimate athlete's modelled disqualification risk only modestly (a Haugen-sized effect, which would matter, is excluded by the measured interval). The same 0.100 s threshold is applied to official RTs whose championship-level offsets span 34 ms; the per-championship fitted probability of a gun-triggered RT below 0.100 s ranges from 2e-06 (95% CI 7.8e-16 to 0.00087) to 5.44 (95% CI 1.22 to 15.86) per 1,000 (men). Whatever their source, these offsets move the rule's implied false-positive rate far more than the starter's interval does. The offsets are not a changed detection threshold (force-trace check, section 1); what causes them is not identified here.

**Assumptions (all stated in fairness_sim.py):**

- Legitimate = gun-triggered; anticipations are not modelled. The fitted RT distributions come from valid championship starts (0.100-0.300 s) and are extrapolated below 0.100 s by a parametric family.
- The hold shifts the whole RT distribution (location only); it does not change its spread or shape.
- Haugen et al. (2013) |r| = 0.16 is an athlete-level correlation; converted to a slope with our within-championship RT SD and the championship hold SD of 0.158 s (Otsuka et al. 2017).
- Hazard scenario: RT is linear in the subjective hazard of the starter's hold distribution (Weber blur phi); athletes know the distribution; k is fixed across starter policies.
- Meet offsets are the championship deviations estimated in descriptive.json and act as pure location shifts.
- Athlete heterogeneity: normal athlete effects (SD from the crossed model); within-athlete ex-Gaussian fitted to conditional residuals; race-to-race variation added as a normal component.
- Parameter uncertainty = race-cluster bootstrap of the reference fit only; scenario inputs (r, slopes, phi, hold distribution) are fixed design values varied as sensitivities, not estimated.

## 5. Systematic (athlete-independent) variation: pre-registered analyses
Plan, estimands and decision rules were committed before computation in `analysis/prereg_systematic.md` (addenda list every deviation). Quantities marked SIMULATION come from models fitted to the data.
*Source:* `analysis/outputs/systematic.json` from `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis/systematic.py --seed 20260928 --descriptive analysis/outputs/descriptive.json --fp-models analysis/outputs/fp_models.json --calibration analysis/outputs/calibration.json --boot-r 2000 --sims 10000 --boot-h2 200 --boot-champ 100 --min-n 100 --min-races 10 --haugen-design analysis/haugen_design.csv --fiore-params analysis/fiore2025_params.csv --fiore-venue-pdf analysis/external/fiore2025/ComparisonOfVenueEffects.pdf --out analysis/outputs/systematic.json`  
*Data:* rt_waveform.csv@sha256:3711f9b56ca6 (277 rows); rt_athletes.csv@sha256:af44975b3e1a (4904 rows); races.csv@sha256:4f0f5097c6dc (625 rows); manual_annotations.csv@sha256:fe3736ebbb0f (36 rows); rt_fiore.csv@sha256:dff5cb184d1b (2805 rows); haugen_design.csv@sha256:d5b502e031f3 (4 rows); foreperiods.csv@sha256:376f05038040 (105 rows); fiore2025_params.csv@sha256:ef7c65a24550 (3 rows); ComparisonOfVenueEffects.pdf@sha256:1254ba381a51

### H1: can championship structure make a null hold effect look like Haugen et al.'s r = 0.16?

Sample: 232 races, 1,778 valid RTs, 4 championships with a validated Seiko Ready Time (the section 2 sample).

- **(a) Within-championship model** (section 2, reproduced): 0.32 (95% CI -0.15 to 0.80) ms per 100 ms, r-equivalent 0.035 (95% CI -0.016 to 0.087); excludes the Haugen-sized 1.47 ms: yes.
- **(b) Same model without championship effects:** 2.40 (95% CI 1.63 to 3.16) ms per 100 ms, r-equivalent 0.245 (95% CI 0.167 to 0.324) (race SD rises to 13.8 ms from 4.9 ms, absorbing the championship offsets).
- **(c) Naive athlete-level Pearson r, championships pooled (Haugen-style):** r = 0.221 (95% CI 0.128 to 0.308) (race-cluster bootstrap CI; the naive Fisher CI a start-level analysis would report is [0.176, 0.265], naive p = 4.4e-21). Centred within championship: r = 0.036 (95% CI -0.036 to 0.105). Secondary: on the audio-calibrated foreperiod scale r = 0.374 (95% CI 0.299 to 0.440); men 0.200 (95% CI 0.052 to 0.333); women 0.231 (95% CI 0.117 to 0.343); 100 m only 0.273 (95% CI 0.108 to 0.445).
- **(d) Championship means (descriptive, no inference):** Pearson r of mean Ready Time and athlete-adjusted RT offset 0.82 over the 4 championships of the sample (Spearman 0.80); 0.26 with WIC2025 added (anomalous Ready Times; Spearman 0.80); 1.00 on the calibrated foreperiod scale (3 championships).

| championship | races | mean Ready Time (s) | RT offset (ms, athlete-adjusted) | RT offset (ms, model a) | calibration offset (s) | mean foreperiod (s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WCH2022 | 66 | 1.480 | -18.4 | -14.5 | 0.274 | 1.754 |
| WCH2023 | 63 | 1.691 | -1.1 | 1.9 | 0.385 | 2.076 |
| WCH2025 | 62 | 1.696 | 15.5 | 18.6 | 0.610 | 2.306 |
| WIC2024 | 41 | 1.472 | -7.1 | -6.0 | n/a | n/a |
| WIC2025 | 38 | 3.247 | 1.3 | n/a | n/a | n/a |

**Structural simulations (SIMULATION; naive pooled r of each simulated dataset):**

- S1, observed structure, true within-championship slope 0: mean r 0.178 (95% of datasets 0.132 to 0.223); P(r >= 0.16) = 0.779; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 0.779.
- S1, true slope = the (a) estimate: mean r 0.201 (95% of datasets 0.155 to 0.246); P(r >= 0.16) = 0.963; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 0.963.
- S1, slope 0, foreperiod scale (championship-specific Ready Time definition shift): mean r 0.315 (95% of datasets 0.267 to 0.361); P(r >= 0.16) = 1.000; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 1.000.
- S1, slope 0, championship-centred r (control): mean r -0.008 (95% of datasets -0.062 to 0.046); P(r >= 0.16) = 0.000; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 0.000.
- S1 control, slope 0 and no championship offsets: mean r -0.012 (95% of datasets -0.067 to 0.043); P(r >= 0.16) = 0.000; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 0.000.
- S2, exchangeable championships, slope 0: mean r -0.008 (95% of datasets -0.178 to 0.162); P(r >= 0.16) = 0.026; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 0.063.
- S2, exchangeable championships, slope = the (a) estimate: mean r 0.024 (95% of datasets -0.143 to 0.194); P(r >= 0.16) = 0.051; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 0.068.
- S3, Haugen et al. 2013 design 'cell5', true effect 0 (primary): mean r -0.001 (95% of datasets -0.255 to 0.256); P(r >= 0.16) = 0.110; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 0.223.
- S3, Haugen et al. 2013 design 'all12', true effect 0: mean r -0.001 (95% of datasets -0.174 to 0.175); P(r >= 0.16) = 0.036; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 0.073.
- S3, Haugen et al. 2013 design 'cell5_heattrunc', true effect 0: mean r -0.0003 (95% of datasets -0.256 to 0.252); P(r >= 0.16) = 0.106; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 0.213.
- S3, Haugen et al. 2013 design 'cell5_litsd', true effect 0: mean r 0.001 (95% of datasets -0.277 to 0.278); P(r >= 0.16) = 0.134; P(|r| >= 0.16) = 0.263.

**H1 verdict (pre-registered rule): SUPPORTS the mechanism** (observed structure). Criteria: W yes, R1 yes, R2 yes, Q1 yes, Q2 no, Q3 yes. Championship-centred r below 0.16: yes.
Stated plainly: with this sample's four championships treated as exchangeable (S2), a Haugen-sized r arose by chance in only 0.026 of simulated datasets, below the 0.05 bar; the association in these data comes from the observed alignment of their championships, not from pooling alone.

### H2: how much do 'human limit' estimates depend on championship structure?

All 6,408 valid RTs; left-truncated fits; barrier = RT with fitted probability 1e-3 below it; race-cluster bootstrap CIs (the adjusted fit also redraws the championship offsets).

| sex | family | pooled P(RT<0.100) | pooled barrier (ms) | adjusted P(RT<0.100) | adjusted barrier (ms) | pooled - adjusted (ms) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M | exgauss | 0.00066 | 101.8 | 0.00036 | 104.3 | -2.5 |
| M | slognorm | 0.0001 | 106.0 | 6.2e-05 | 107.5 | -1.5 |
| M | swald | 9.6e-05 | 106.1 | 6.2e-05 | 107.5 | -1.4 |
| W | exgauss | 0.00039 | 104.1 | 0.00015 | 107.4 | -3.3 |
| W | slognorm | 2.4e-05 | 108.5 | 7.9e-06 | 110.5 | -2.1 |
| W | swald | 2e-05 | 108.5 | 7e-06 | 110.4 | -1.9 |

- **Per championship, sex M** (18 eligible): ex-Gaussian barrier from 93.5 ms (WCH2022) to 123.6 ms (WCH2013), a 30.2 ms spread; between-championship SD net of sampling error 6.9 ms (95% prediction width 27.2 ms; Cochran Q p = 2.5e-11); shifted lognormal 30.6 ms, shifted Wald 30.6 ms. Post-hoc, with round and event composition removed: 26.4 ms.
- **Family-to-family spread, sex M:** pooled 4.2 ms, adjusted 3.3 ms, per-championship median 4.1 ms; the pooled P(RT<0.100) differs 6.9-fold across families.
- **Per championship, sex W** (12 eligible): ex-Gaussian barrier from 92.1 ms (WCH2022) to 121.3 ms (WCH2025), a 29.2 ms spread; between-championship SD net of sampling error 6.3 ms (95% prediction width 24.7 ms; Cochran Q p = 6.2e-06); shifted lognormal 25.4 ms, shifted Wald 24.6 ms. Post-hoc, with round and event composition removed: 31.4 ms.
- **Family-to-family spread, sex W:** pooled 4.4 ms, adjusted 3.1 ms, per-championship median 4.6 ms; the pooled P(RT<0.100) differs 19.3-fold across families.
- **Stated plainly:** the pooled barrier lies only 2.5 ms below the championship-adjusted one (men, ex-Gaussian; pooled minus adjusted -2.5 (95% CI -3.9 to -0.1) ms), below the 10 ms bar, because removing the offsets mainly shortens the fitted exponential (right) tail (tau 16.7 -> 14.5 ms) while the Gaussian SD that sets the left tail barely moves (sigma 14.0 -> 13.7 ms). Pooling therefore does not bias the single pooled estimate much; what the offsets change is the limit that would be estimated from any one championship.

**H2 verdict (pre-registered rule, men / ex-Gaussian): CONSEQUENTIAL** (C1 per-championship spread with noise guard: yes; C2 pooled-vs-adjusted shift >= 10 ms: no). Other cells: M_slognorm CONSEQUENTIAL; M_swald CONSEQUENTIAL; W_exgauss CONSEQUENTIAL; W_slognorm CONSEQUENTIAL; W_swald CONSEQUENTIAL.


## 5b. Trend analyses (pre-registered: `analysis/prereg_addendum_trend.md`)
*Source:* `analysis/outputs/trend.json` from `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis/trend.py --seed 20260928 --descriptive analysis/outputs/descriptive.json --calibration analysis/outputs/calibration.json --fairness analysis/outputs/fairness.json --sims 20000 --boot 5000 --perm 10000 --out analysis/outputs/trend.json`  
*Data:* rt_athletes.csv@sha256:af44975b3e1a (4904 rows); rt_fiore.csv@sha256:dff5cb184d1b (2805 rows); races.csv@sha256:4f0f5097c6dc (625 rows); manual_annotations.csv@sha256:fe3736ebbb0f (36 rows); rt_waveform.csv@sha256:3711f9b56ca6 (277 rows); foreperiods.csv@sha256:376f05038040 (105 rows)

### H1-S4: which analysis makes Haugen et al.'s reported cells likely? (SIMULATION)

- At the largest within-championship effect our data allow (r_w = 0.113), a within-championship analysis produces the reported pattern with probability 0.0028, a pooled analysis with probability 0.116; likelihood ratio 42 (11 when the non-significant women 2003-2009 cell is added to the pattern).
- At our estimate (r_w = 0.039): 9.5e-07 vs 0.018 (likelihood ratio 18,460).
- The within-championship effect that would make the pattern plausible (P >= 0.05): r = 0.155, against a conservative upper bound of 0.113 from our data.

**Verdicts:** WITHIN-CHAMPIONSHIP ANALYSIS STATISTICALLY IMPLAUSIBLE (primary rule); likelihood rule FAVOURS POOLED; inversion rule WITHIN READING NEEDS r_w ABOVE OUR CI. Stated plainly: with the pooled side evaluated at our estimate the rule reads INCONCLUSIVE (pattern improbable under both analyses), and at r_w = 0 INCONCLUSIVE (pattern improbable under both analyses). This is a statement about probabilities under a reconstructed design, not about what Haugen et al. did.

*Caveat:* Before 2010 a false start did not disqualify outright (pre-2003: DQ on an athlete's own second false start; 2003-2009: first false start in a race charged to the field), so anticipation, and with it the within-championship hold effect in 1997-2009, may genuinely differ from ours (2022-2025, zero tolerance); r_star states how large it would have had to be.

### H6: is championship-level variation getting worse?

- **SD of athlete-adjusted offsets by period:** pre-2010 8.0 (95% CI 3.2 to 11.0) ms (6 championships); 2010-2019 4.9 (95% CI 1.2 to 6.4) ms (5 championships); 2020-2025 12.2 (95% CI 2.2 to 16.9) ms (5 championships).
- **Ratio 2020-2025 / pre-2010:** 1.52 (95% CI 0.31 to 3.75) (bootstrap; F-based CI [0.56, 4.66]). **Verdict: PERSISTING, NOT WORSENING.** Five or six championships per period cannot detect a moderate increase: 'not worsening' means no credible increase, not evidence of stability.
- **Trend** in the absolute deviation from a linear time trend: 0.42 (95% CI -2.65 to 3.89) ms per decade (permutation p = 0.79); plain |offset|: -0.24 (95% CI -3.28 to 3.40) (p = 0.89). Fastest and slowest offsets: WCH2022 -18.4 ms and WCH2025 15.5 ms (both in 2020-2025).
- **Modelled legitimate sub-0.100 s starts per 1,000 (SIMULATION), period medians:** pre-2010 0.76 (95% CI 0.35 to 1.53) (span 245-fold); 2010-2019 0.07 (95% CI 0.02 to 0.13) (span 24-fold); 2020-2025 0.58 (95% CI 0.29 to 1.31) (span 4,430-fold).
- **Recorded false starts with RT 0.090-0.100 s:** 4 of 27 false starts with an RT (6,452 starts), by championship: WCH2022 4.

*Caveat:* False-start recording differs by source: from 2015 official PDF labels (TR16.8) plus RT; 1999-2013 rows come from Fiore et al.'s file, where a false start is visible only as an RT below 0.100 s. Near-threshold counts therefore compare across championships of one source only.

## 5c. Guard band for the 0.100 s rule (pre-registered: `analysis/prereg_guardband.md`)
*Source:* `analysis/outputs/guardband.json` from `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis/guardband.py --seed 20260928 --descriptive analysis/outputs/descriptive.json --fairness analysis/outputs/fairness.json --offset-draws 25 --out analysis/outputs/guardband.json`  
*Data:* rt_athletes.csv@sha256:af44975b3e1a (4904 rows); rt_fiore.csv@sha256:dff5cb184d1b (2805 rows); races.csv@sha256:4f0f5097c6dc (625 rows); rt_waveform.csv@sha256:3711f9b56ca6 (277 rows); manual_annotations.csv@sha256:fe3736ebbb0f (36 rows)

- **Band:** u = 9.3 (95% CI 6.6 to 13.1) ms (between-championship SD of athlete-adjusted offsets) gives g = 15.2 ms at k = 1.645 (9.3 at k = 1, 18.5 at k = 2): a limit of 0.0848 s (0.085 s on the RT grid).
- **(a) Modelled legitimate starts scored below the limit, per 1,000 (SIMULATION, men, ex-Gaussian):**
  - P0 current rule: highest 13.83 (95% CI 8.61 to 20.22) (WCH2022; draw median 13.51), median 0.29 (95% CI 0.14 to 0.57), highest / median 48 (95% CI 26 to 88).
  - P1 uniform guard band: highest 0.62 (95% CI 0.27 to 1.15) (WCH2022; draw median 0.58), median 0.0035 (95% CI 0.0011 to 0.01), highest / median 176 (95% CI 77 to 415); cut vs P0 22.4 (95% CI 17.0 to 31.9)-fold.
  - P2 per-championship calibration (draw medians): highest 0.51 (95% CI 0.27 to 1.07), median 0.27 (95% CI 0.14 to 0.50), highest / median 1.9 (95% CI 1.4 to 3.3); cut vs P0 26.1 (95% CI 13.6 to 47.0)-fold.
  - P3 calibration plus residual band (draw medians): highest 0.28 (95% CI 0.14 to 0.57), median 0.15 (95% CI 0.08 to 0.31), highest / median 1.8 (95% CI 1.4 to 2.9); cut vs P0 47.9 (95% CI 26.0 to 82.8)-fold.
  - Stated plainly: a uniform band lowers every championship's rate but widens the highest-to-median spread, because it moves every championship deeper into the left tail; only per-championship calibration (P2, P3) equalises.
- **(b) Recorded false starts no longer flagged:** 4 of 27 with a measured RT under P1 (WCH2022 4); P2 4, P3 4; legal starts newly flagged under P2: 1.
- **(c) Cost share:** 0.148 (95% CI 0.059 to 0.325) of recorded false starts with a measured RT fall inside the band (5 at or above the limit).

**Verdict:** TRADE-OFF: PROTECTIVE BUT CHANGES MORE THAN 10% OF RECORDED FALSE STARTS. At k = 1 the cut is 5.8 (95% CI 5.0 to 7.1)-fold; a 10-fold cut needs g >= 12.0 ms while changing at most 10% of recorded false starts allows g <= 4.5 ms - no band meets both.

*Caveat:* SIMULATION: every rate in (a) is a model output. Legitimate = gun-triggered; the reference RT distribution is fitted to valid championship starts (0.100-0.300 s, left-truncated) and extrapolated below 0.100 s; the family changes absolute rates several-fold (sensitivity cells).

## 6. Confounder audit of published RT effects (pre-registered, addendum 3)
Rules fixed before computation (`analysis/prereg_systematic.md`, addendum 3; post-hoc diagnostics in addendum 5 are labelled). H1-S4 and H6 of addendum 4 are delegated to `analysis/trend.py`.

### H3: rule-era contrasts depend on which championships represent each era

Athlete-adjusted offsets; eras pre-2003 (2 WCH), 2003-2009 (4 WCH), 2010+ (10 outdoor championships); within-era between-championship SD 9.1 ms.

- **2003-2009 minus pre-2003:** 12.2 (95% CI 9.0 to 15.4) ms with all championships; no subset variation (both eras use all their available championships); random-championship P(opposite sign) 0.021 with 5 vs 5 championships.
- **2010+ minus 2003-2009 (zero tolerance; primary):** 1.9 (95% CI -0.2 to 4.1) ms with all championships; Haugen-matched designs range -13.3 to 14.0 ms (opposite sign in 36% of 45 designs); random-championship P(opposite sign) 0.402 with 2 vs 5 championships.
- **2010+ minus pre-2003:** 14.1 (95% CI 11.2 to 17.1) ms with all championships; Haugen-matched designs range -1.2 to 26.2 ms (opposite sign in 2% of 45 designs); random-championship P(opposite sign) 0.035 with 2 vs 5 championships.
- **2010+ minus all pre-2010:** 6.0 (95% CI 4.0 to 7.9) ms with all championships; Haugen-matched designs range -9.3 to 18.1 ms (opposite sign in 18% of 45 designs); random-championship P(opposite sign) 0.085 with 11 vs 8 championships.
- **Same-rule null (no rule change; 2 vs 5 zero-tolerance championships):** 95% of 'era contrasts' within -18.0 to 15.4 ms (half-width 16.7); for Han et al.'s sizes (11 vs 8) the half-width is 8.3 ms. One championship standing for zero tolerance: WCH2011 gives 24.2 ms and WCH2022 -6.9 ms against 1999/2001.
- Sensitivity, 2010+ minus 2003-2009: raw means 2.9 ms (opposite sign in 31% of designs); with World Indoors 1.0 ms (42%).

**H3 verdicts:** SAMPLING CAN FLIP THE SIGN; Haugen et al.'s +30 ms: EXCEEDS SAME-RULE NOISE; Han et al.'s -4 ms: WITHIN SAME-RULE NOISE.

### H4: the sex gap by championship

- **All events (primary):** average gap (women minus men) 4.0 (95% CI 2.3 to 5.8) ms; per-championship gaps from -11.9 (WCH2007) to 14.5 ms (WCH2013); interaction LRT p = 6.4e-08; between-championship SD 4.8 ms, 95% prediction interval [-5.3, 13.3] ms; 4 of 18 championships with women faster.
- **Flat sprints only (secondary):** average gap (women minus men) 7.4 (95% CI 4.9 to 10.0) ms; per-championship gaps from -2.5 (WCH1999) to 13.5 ms (WCH2013); interaction LRT p = 0.045; between-championship SD 2.7 ms, 95% prediction interval [2.0, 12.8] ms; 2 of 18 championships with women faster.
- **POST-HOC, 2015 on (athlete-identified):** average gap (women minus men) 7.7 (95% CI 5.4 to 9.9) ms; per-championship gaps from 3.2 (OG2020) to 11.1 ms (WCH2025); interaction LRT p = 0.052; between-championship SD 1.6 ms, 95% prediction interval [4.6, 10.7] ms; 0 of 10 championships with women faster.

**H4 verdict (all events):** CHAMPIONSHIP-DEPENDENT (sign not stable). Stated plainly: all 4 championships with women faster are before 2010 (a different source table, with other round and event composition); the championships from 2015 on show none; with flat sprints only the prediction interval excludes 0.

### H5: venue dependence inside Fiore et al.'s own published model

Parameters transcribed from their manuscript source (`analysis/fiore2025_params.csv`, checked against `analysis/external/fiore2025/source_extract.txt`); quadrature instead of their 10^7 draws.

- **men incl. 2022 (primary):** marginal P(RT<0.100) 0.00278 (published 0.00276); 1e-3 barrier 93.9 ms (published 94); median venue 96.5 ms; 2.5th vs 97.5th percentile venue 86.1 vs 108.1 ms (range 22.0 ms).
- **men excl. 2022:** marginal P(RT<0.100) 0.00239 (published 0.00194); 1e-3 barrier 94.7 ms (published 96); median venue 96.1 ms; 2.5th vs 97.5th percentile venue 88.4 vs 104.6 ms (range 16.2 ms).
- **women:** marginal P(RT<0.100) 0.00054 (published 0.000546); 1e-3 barrier 102.0 ms (published 102); median venue 107.0 ms; 2.5th vs 97.5th percentile venue 95.7 vs 119.6 ms (range 24.0 ms).
- **The 13 actual WCH venues** (effects read from their vector figure): barrier from 84.7 ms (2022) to 106.4 ms (2011), a 21.7 ms range.
- **POST-HOC:** the excluding-2022 row's published tail implies an intercept of -1.897 (printed -1.910); their figure's panels differ by 0.0109 on the log scale, the shift removing 2022 implies. The other two sets imply their printed intercepts (-1.909, -1.921).

**H5 verdicts:** NOT REPRODUCED (men_incl2022 True; men_excl2022 False; women True); VENUE MATERIAL WITHIN FIORE ET AL.'S MODEL.

### O2 (optional): RT vs 100 m time, pooled vs within championship

- **Men:** pooled r 0.23 (95% CI 0.12 to 0.33); championship-centred 0.26 (95% CI 0.17 to 0.34); within race 0.14 (95% CI 0.06 to 0.23); pooled minus centred -0.03 (95% CI -0.09 to 0.04).
- **Women:** pooled r 0.25 (95% CI 0.14 to 0.35); championship-centred 0.32 (95% CI 0.22 to 0.39); within race 0.25 (95% CI 0.14 to 0.36); pooled minus centred -0.07 (95% CI -0.11 to -0.01).

**O2 verdict:** NOT CONFOUNDED BY CHAMPIONSHIP.


## 7. EXPLORATORY probe of the recent championships (NOT pre-registered)
Every number in this section is exploratory: chosen after looking at the data, with no pre-registered rule. Produced by `analysis/recent_probe.py` and published as `probe.*` by `analysis/probe_numbers.py`; the full probe is in `analysis/outputs/recent_probe.json` and `notes/recent_probe.md`.
*Source:* `analysis/outputs/probe.json` from `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis/probe_numbers.py --probe analysis/outputs/recent_probe.json --out analysis/outputs/probe.json`  
*Data:* rt_athletes.csv@sha256:af44975b3e1a (4904 rows); rt_fiore.csv@sha256:dff5cb184d1b (2805 rows); races.csv@sha256:4f0f5097c6dc (625 rows); rt_waveform_lanes.csv@sha256:0559f4575d94 (2493 rows); extra_rt.csv@sha256:14a3c938e798 (1593 rows); combined_rt.csv@sha256:9b83b753d122 (430 rows); gun_features.csv@sha256:ce91c6cfa253 (123 rows); gun_spectra_peaks.csv@sha256:445530ab6c8d (7 rows); gun_spectra_similarity.csv@sha256:aded84d69025 (7 rows)

- **WCH2025, start location:** the same athletes were 22.2 (95% CI 15.2 to 29.0) ms slower on the home-straight start than on the 200 m start (24 athletes); at the other championships the same difference ranged from -9.2 (95% CI -14.9 to -3.1) ms (WCH2015) to 4.5 (95% CI -0.4 to 10.1) ms (WCH2023).
- **WCH2025, lanes:** on the home-straight start, run on 2025-09-13 to 2025-09-16, RT rose by 2.36 (95% CI 1.56 to 3.13) ms per lane outwards; lanes 7-9 minus lanes 1-5 12.2 (95% CI 8.1 to 16.6) ms.
- **WCH2022, everywhere:** faster than WCH2023 in 3 of 3 rounds and 2 of 2 start types; 8 of 8 competition days below every WCH2023 day; the shift was near-uniform across the distribution (median change minus 5th-percentile change -2.1 (95% CI -5.2 to 1.7) ms).
- **Same athletes:** 68 athletes at all three recent World Championships slowed by 15.4 (95% CI 12.3 to 18.5) ms from 2022 to 2023 and by 16.7 (95% CI 11.6 to 21.6) ms from 2023 to 2025.

## 8. Literature audit: published championship-RT designs exposed to championship-level variation
Protocol committed before coding (`lit/audit_protocol.md`); a structured search, not an exhaustive one. The audit classifies designs, not the truth of findings.
*Source:* `analysis/outputs/lit_audit.json` from `.venv\Scripts\python.exe analysis/lit_audit.py --studies lit/audit_studies.csv --screening lit/audit_screening.csv --search-log lit/audit_search/search_log.json --out analysis/outputs/lit_audit.json`  
*Data:* audit_studies.csv@sha256:5e48a95daeec (89 rows); audit_screening.csv@sha256:ecd2a0e96514 (558 rows)

- **Exposed designs:** 28 of 40 studies (70%) drew at least one conclusion from a design that pooled, contrasted or generalised championships without modelling them; 50 of 76 findings (66%). Counting only conclusions that rest on a between-championship contrast: 22 studies.
- **Sensitivities:** unclear designs counted as exposed 34 studies; design coding verified from full text or abstract 28 of 37; with grey literature 35 of 52.
- **Conflicting literatures:** 6 groups (lane, performance_correlation, round, rule_era, sex, threshold_limit). Rule era: 10 of 10 studies exposed; every rule-era finding rests on an era contrast with championships nested in eras.
- **Context:** 9 studies report an RT difference between championships as their own finding; 10 analyse a single championship; 9 were closed access; 558 unique records screened.

*Caveat:* Counts classify designs, not the truth of findings; the search is not exhaustive and is English-dominant; closed full texts were coded from abstracts or secondary sources.

## Figures
Figures are in `analysis/figures/` (PNG + PDF), each rendered by `analysis/make_figures.py` (commands in producers.json). The abstract allows two: ★ marks the candidates.

- ★ `analysis/figures/fairness.png` — Simulated legitimate false-start risk: (A) across Seiko Ready Times under the measured slope (with 95% CI) and |r| = 0.16 alternatives, with the observed Ready Times as ticks; (B) across championship RT offsets, with recorded false-start rates (championships without a diamond had none recorded with an RT).
- ★ `analysis/figures/holds.png` — Seiko Ready Time vs championship-adjusted race-mean RT (232 races, 4 championships; marker area = valid RTs in the race) with the linear fit (95% CI), a df-3 spline and Haugen-sized reference slopes; championship RT offsets 1999-2025.
- `analysis/figures/power.png` — Power to detect |r| = 0.16 by number of races and residual race ICC; minimum detectable |r|.
- `analysis/figures/rt_hist.png` — Distribution of championship RTs by sex around the 0.100 s threshold.
- `analysis/figures/terciles.png` — Mean RT and share of RTs < 0.120 s by within-championship Seiko Ready Time tercile.
- `analysis/figures/policies.png` — Starter hold policies under the hazard model: mean RT change vs legitimate false-start risk.
- ★ `analysis/figures/story_wide.png` — Abstract Figure 1: RT shift across the 10th-90th percentile Seiko Ready Time (measured slope with 95% CI, and a Haugen-sized slope) beside the athlete-adjusted championship offsets (95% CIs), on one RT scale.
- ★ `analysis/figures/confound.png` — Confounder audit, three panels: (A) the Seiko Ready Time-RT correlation in the same 232 races, pooled over championships (Haugen-style) and centred within championship (race-cluster bootstrap 95% CIs), against Haugen et al.'s r = 0.16; (B) the limit moves with the championship: each championship's 1-in-1,000 barrier (men, ex-Gaussian, bootstrap 95% CIs), sorted, coloured below/above the 0.100 s rule (5 of 18 below), spanning 93-124 ms, against the shaded band between the published proposals 0.094 s (Fiore et al. 2025) and 0.119 s (Brosnan et al. 2017); (C) the modelled rate of legitimate starts wrongly disqualified per 1,000 at each championship (log scale; SIMULATION) under the current rule, a uniform guard band and per-championship calibration, with the guard band's cost (4 of 27 recorded false starts no longer flagged, at WCH2022, RT 0.093-0.099 s). Compact layout (7.0 x 3.2 in): B and C label only Eugene 2022 (WCH2022, ringed) and the championship at the far end. Registered but not drawn: championships whose barrier CI contains 0.100 s: 4; 0.094 s: 5; 0.119 s: 4; none: 9.

## Negative and null results (state plainly)
- No detectable linear effect of Seiko Ready Time on mean RT within championships: 0.32 (95% CI -0.15 to 0.80) ms per 100 ms (232 races). This is absence of evidence bounded by the CI; the design detects 0.68 ms per 100 ms with 80% power.
- Predictors whose estimated effect has the opposite sign to their theory's prediction: h_obj_comp_z, h_subj_comp_z, h_abort_comp_z, fmtp_cdf_comp_z (see section 2). A lower AIC for such a model is not support for its theory.
- No detectable association of the share of RTs below 0.120 s with Ready Time (odds ratio per 100 ms 0.99 (95% CI 0.90 to 1.10); permutation p = 0.829).
- The Omega vs Seiko contrast is uninformative (few championships per vendor).
- Tail probabilities below 0.100 s are extrapolations from truncated data and differ by family (section 1).
- Not estimable: false-start probability as a function of the interval, because the interval of the aborted attempt is not in the waveform data. Not run: a dispersion (scale) model.
- The Ready Time -> foreperiod calibration rests on 22 audio-annotated races.
